13.1- Homeostasis
Exercise	1.	Which term covers the rest of the three:
(A)Osmoregulation(B)Excretion(C)Thermoregulation(D)Homeostasis
Additional	2.	The maintenance of a stable internal environment is called:
(A)Thermoregulation(B)Osmoregulation(C)Homeostasis(D)Respiration
Additional	3.	Homeostasis helps living organisms maintain a stable:
(A)External environment(B)Internal environment(C)Atmospheric pressure(D)Body shape only
Additional	4.	The basic mechanism of homeostasis involves how many essential elements:
(A)Two(B)Three(C)Four(D)Five
Additional	5.	Which of the following is NOT an essential element of homeostasis:
(A)Receptors(B)Control centre(C)Effectors(D)Chromosomes
Additional	6.	Receptors are also known as:
(A)Effectors(B)Enzymes(C)Hormones(D)Sensors
Additional	7.	Which is responsible for detecting changes in the internal or external environment:
(A)Control Centre(B)Effectors(C)Receptors(D)Set points
Additional	8.	Receptors transmit information to the:
(A)Effectors(B)Muscles(C)Control centre(D)Kidneys
Additional	9.	Receptor cells in the skin detect changes in:
(A)Blood sugar(B)Temperature(C)Oxygen level(D)Heart rate
Additional	10.	The control centre receives information from:
(A)Hormones only(B)Receptors(C)Muscles only(D)Bones only
Additional	11.	Which organ acts as a control centre in homeostasis:
(A)Brain(B)Stomach(C)Kidney only(D)Skin only
Additional	12.	Which part of the nervous system acts as a control centre:
(A)Spinal cord(B)Rib cage(C)Femur(D)Pancreas
Additional	13.	Which glands also act as control centres in homeostasis:
(A)Sweat glands(B)Endocrine glands(C)Salivary glands(D)Sebaceous glands
Additional	14.	Parts of the body that respond to messages from the control centreare called:
(A)Control Centre(B)Set points(C)Receptors(D)Effectors
Additional	15.	Effectors respond when they receive messages from the:
(A)Receptors(B)Blood vessels(C)Control centres(D)Endocrine glands
Additional	16.	Which of the following acts as an effector:
(A)Muscles and glands(B)Bones and cartilage(C)Arteries and veins(D)Brain and spinal cord
Additional	17.	The response of effectors helps restore the internal environment to its:
(A)Maximum temperature(B)Set points(C)Highest pressure(D)Lowest pH
Additional	18.	Feedback mechanisms are the foundation of:
(A)Digestion(B)Homeostasis(C)Respiration(D)Circulation
Additional	19.	The output of a system is used to control the behavior of the system itself is called:
(A)Excretion(B)Osmoregulation(C)Respiration(D)Feedback mechanism
Additional	20.	How many types of feedback mechanisms are there:
(A)One(B)Three(C)Two(D)Four
Additional	21.	The mechanism in which the output reduces the activity of the system is called:
(A)Negative feedback(B)Positive feedback(C)Neutral feedback(D)Active feedback
Additional	22.	Regulation of body temperature is an example of :
(A)Negative feedback(B)Positive feedback(C)Neutral feedback(D)Active feedback
Additional	23.	What is the normal body temperature set point :
(A)35°C(B)36°C(C)37°C(D)38°C
Additional	24.	Which receptors detect an increase in body temperature:
(A)Photoreceptors(B)Thermoreceptors(C)Chemoreceptors(D)Mechanoreceptors
Additional	25.	Thermoreceptors responsible for temperature regulation are located in the:
(A)Heart(B)Hypothalamus of the brain(C)Kidneys(D)Liver
Additional	26.	When body temperature rises above 37°C, the control centre sends messages to:
(A)Sweat glands(B)Bones(C)Lungs(D)Kidneys
Additional	27.	Sweating causes body temperature to:
(A)Increase(B)Decrease(C)Remain unchanged(D)Reach 40°C
Additional	28.	In temperature regulation, sweat glands act as:
(A)Receptors(B)Control centres(C)Effectors(D)Hormones
Additional	29.	A mechanism in which the output increases the activity of the system is called:
(A)Negative feedback(B)Active feedback(C)Neutral feedback(D)Positive feedback
Additional	30.	Which process is regulated by positive feedback:
(A)Childbirth(B)Digestion(C)Respiration(D)Excretion
Additional	31.	In childbirth, the control centre sends signals to the:
(A)Thyroid gland(B)Pituitary gland(C)Adrenal gland(D)Pancreas
Additional	32.	Which hormone is released by the pituitary gland during childbirth:
(A)Insulin(B)Adrenaline(C)Oxytocin(D)Thyroxine
Additional	33.	Severe inflammation or infection causes immune cells to release:
(A)Enzymes(B)Cytokines(C)Antibodies only(D)Hemoglobin
Additional	34.	The proteins released by immune cells during inflammation are called:
(A)Cytokines(B)Antibodies(C)Hemoglobin(D)Albumin
Additional	35.	Which of the following is an example of positive feedback:
(A)Sweat glands(B)Osmoregulation(C)Lungs(D)Blood clotting
